TITLE OF THE SERIES
FISSION VOID — The Demon Hunter’s Steed

CHAPTER / PIECE TITLE
Prologue — The Ghost in the Machine

IMAGE TYPE IDENTIFICATION
Single unified full-bleed 9:16 poster composition. No panels. No gutters. One continuous image with the visual and emotional force of a dark mythic tapestry rendered in hyper‑realistic, cinematic photography.

ART STYLE DECLARATION — SACRED MANTRA
4K, 8K, HD, UHD, hyperdetailed, raw photo, cinematic lighting, anamorphic lens, sharp focus, intricate surface texture, subsurface scattering on painted metal and translucent materials, visible micro‑scratches and natural imperfections, faint peach fuzz on leather surfaces, film grain, color graded, ARRI Alexa look, 85mm lens, f/1.8 aperture, masterpiece quality.

The visual identity is that of a high‑budget cinematic advertisement for a futuristic hyperbike — every surface rendered with obsessive fidelity. The lighting is dramatic and directional, with deep shadows that carve out the bike’s aggressive lines and soft highlights that kiss the edges of its armored panels. The color grading leans into a cold, desaturated palette with deep navy blues, icy ceruleans, bone whites, and touches of antique bronze, all anchored by the pulsing azure glow of the bike’s energy core and headlights. Film grain is present at 5% intensity, evenly distributed, adding texture without degrading the hyper‑detailed finish. The image must feel as though it was captured on a cinema camera with an anamorphic lens — slight oval bokeh in out‑of‑focus background elements, a subtle horizontal lens flare, and a shallow depth of field that keeps the bike razor‑sharp while the cathedral ruins behind it soften into atmospheric abstraction. Every material reads as real: cold metal, warm leather, painted carbon fiber, translucent glass over LEDs, and the faint mist of arcane energy that clings to the ground.

The following elements are absolutely forbidden: 90s anime, cel animation, retro anime, VHS grain, scanlines, hand‑drawn line art, cel shading, pixelation, vector art, low resolution, flat colors, or any descriptor that suggests non‑photorealistic rendering.

CHARACTER REFERENCES — COMPLETE AND EXHAUSTIVE
Not applicable — no character appears. The bike is the sole subject. The following bike design references are locked for all elements of the image.

Primary Bike — FISSION VOID (Central Figure)

Overall Silhouette: A heavy‑armored, futuristic superbike with a broad, muscular stance. The wheelbase is long, the front fork rake is aggressive, and the overall shape is low and predatory — a blend of a demon hunter’s coffin and a fighter jet. The bike is bulkier than a racing superbike, with thick fairings, reinforced shoulders, and an armored presence that suggests it could survive a demon’s claw swipe.

Color Palette: Deep navy blue (almost black in shadows) as the primary base. Cerulean blue as accent stripes and energy lines. Bone white for the skeletal fairing details and exhaust pipes. Antique bronze for trim, bolts, and the crossguard motif. Icy blue for all lighting (headlights, core, rim LEDs, afterimage glows).

Front View: The front fairing is a broad, angular interpretation of a demon skull — two large, vertical icy‑blue LED headlights serve as the “eyes,” each shaped like a narrow, predatory slit. Between them, a silver metallic grille mimics a skeletal nasal cavity and snarling teeth. Above the headlights, two small wing‑like protrusions (bronze) angle backward like horns. The windshield is a dark, smoked polycarbonate visor with a faint blue reflection of cathedral arches. The front fender is split into two sharp, claw‑like points, each tipped with a bronze cap. Below the fairing, a carbon‑fiber splitter with serrated edges extends forward.

Side Profile (Left Side): The fuel tank is heavily armored with overlapping plates that mimic a ribcage — each plate has a thin cerulean LED strip tracing its contour. Behind the front wheel, the side fairing has a deep cutout revealing the Fission Core Engine — a compact turbine‑hybrid block with a transparent azure glass cover, inside which a pulsing blue energy core rotates slowly. The exhaust system is twin rectangular thrust‑vector pipes stacked vertically on the right side (visible from left as they protrude rearward), made of bone‑white ceramic with glowing blue energy rings at the outlets. The swingarm is a massive, single‑sided unit forged from dark titanium, shaped to mimic a demon’s forearm with segmented armor plates. The rear wheel hub has a glowing blue ring that pulses in rhythm with the engine core. The seat is a single piece of black leather with cerulean stitching, low and contoured.

Rear View: The tail unit rises into a sharp, blade‑like hump that terminates in a horizontal LED taillight bar (icy blue, not red). Below the tail, a long, segmented cloak‑panel extends downward — a flexible, articulated panel of navy blue carbon fiber edged with cerulean light, mimicking Alucard’s duster coat tails. This panel has small bronze wing‑shaped ornaments at each segment joint. The turn signals are ultra‑thin amber slivers embedded in the tail’s sides. The license plate recess is shaped like a coffin, hidden under the cloak panel.

Engine Detail (used in afterimage): The Fission Core is a parallel‑twin turbine‑hybrid engine. The cylinder block is finished in dark bronze with cerulean cooling fins. The cam cover is a sculpted silver piece shaped like a skull with glowing blue eyes. Through the transparent azure clutch cover, the clutch basket is visible — its fingers are shaped like miniature longswords. Glowing blue coolant lines snake around the engine block.

Wheels and Tires: Front and rear wheels have six thick, wing‑shaped aerodynamic spokes made of forged magnesium, painted navy blue with silver edges. The rim edges have a continuous icy‑blue LED strip. Tires are high‑performance semi‑slicks (Pirelli Diablo Supercorsa style) with a single blue pinstripe. Brake calipers are six‑piston monobloc units painted antique bronze, gripping wave‑pattern rotors with cutouts shaped like crossguards.

Lighting Consistency: All icy‑blue lighting (headlights, core, rim LEDs, taillight) shares the same cool blue hue — hex approximate #2F6EFF — with a subtle pulsing effect (5% brightness oscillation) simulating a heartbeat or energy cycle.

PANEL LAYOUT AND COMPOSITION MAP
Not applicable — this is a full-bleed unified poster, not a multi-panel composition.

PANEL‑BY‑PANEL NARRATIVE, DIALOGUE AND SPEECH BUBBLES
Not applicable — no panels. The following describes the single unified composition from top to bottom.

The Canvas (9:16 vertical):
Upper Quarter — The Afterimage Array (Bike Part Studies)

Filling the upper portion of the background, from approximately the top edge down to the main bike’s top fairing level, are four ghosted afterimages of the Fission Void’s critical components. These afterimages are rendered semi‑transparent (approximately 40% opacity), floating in the ruined cathedral air like holographic schematics or arcane visions. Each afterimage is labeled with small, elegant calligraphic text integrated into the ink‑wash aesthetic (but rendered in sharp focus, not hand‑drawn). Each afterimage shows a different part of the bike in exquisite detail.

Afterimage 1 (far left) — The Fission Core Engine:
A close‑up, cutaway view of the engine block. The turbine‑hybrid core is visible through its transparent azure cover — a glowing blue vortex of energy suspended between magnetic coils. The cam cover skull grins, its blue eyes bright. Bronze cooling fins radiate outward. A single blue coolant line with a tiny droplet of condensation is visible.
Label (calligraphic, small, floating below the afterimage): “FISSION CORE — Turbine‑Hybrid 1200cc”

Afterimage 2 (center‑left) — Skull Fairing & Headlights:
A tight frontal view of the bike’s nose. The two vertical icy‑blue LED headlights are active, casting beams forward. The silver skeletal grille teeth are sharp. The bronze horn‑like winglets angle back. The smoked windshield reflects a shattered rose window.
Label: “SKULL FAIRING — Active Aero & LED Array”

Afterimage 3 (center‑right) — Cloak Panel & Tail Section:
A rear three‑quarter close‑up of the bike’s tail. The segmented cloak‑panel extends downward, its cerulean edge lights glowing. The horizontal taillight bar is a solid blue line. The coffin‑shaped license plate recess is visible underneath.
Label: “CLOAK PANEL — Segmented Carbon Fiber / Demon Hunter Duster”

Afterimage 4 (far right) — Wing Spoke Wheel & Brake Assembly:
A macro view of the front wheel. The six wing‑shaped spokes are sharp and aerodynamic. The icy‑blue rim LED strip glows. The bronze six‑piston caliper clamps a wave‑pattern rotor with crossguard cutouts. Tire tread is visible with its blue pinstripe.
Label: “WING SPOKE WHEEL — Magnesium / Self‑Illuminating Rim”

All afterimages are positioned so they do not obstruct the central bike but instead frame it, creating a sense of technical reverence and mythic depth.

Middle‑Upper Background — Neo‑Ukiyo‑e and Sumi‑e Ink Wash Environment (Photorealistic Rendering)

Behind and between the afterimages, the background is a hyper‑realistic interpretation of a ruined gothic cathedral under an eternal eclipse. The style is not painted but photographed — yet the composition follows the principles of Japanese ink wash painting: sweeping, wind‑blown arcs of mist and shadow, dramatic contrast, and a sense of ancient, sacred decay.

The cathedral’s skeletal remains include:

Towering pointed arches with crumbling stone ribs.

A massive shattered rose window in the distance, its stained glass fragments still clinging to the iron frame, backlit by the dim glow of the eclipsed sun.

Floating pillars and broken flying buttresses suspended in mid‑air, defying gravity.

Swirling mists of blue arcane energy that curl around the stonework like living smoke.

The sky is a deep bruised purple, with a single ring of light around the black eclipse — a cold, pale corona that casts long, dramatic shadows.

The color palette of the background is desaturated: dusty greys, cold purples, faded golds, and deep indigos. The blue arcane mists provide the only saturated accents besides the bike’s own lights. The overall mood is epic, sorrowful, and vengeful — a cathedral that fell during a demonic invasion.

Lower Two‑Thirds — The Central Figure (Fission Void Superbike)

The bike is parked on the cracked marble floor of the cathedral, approximately in the center of the lower two‑thirds of the frame. The ground is littered with small fragments of colored glass from the rose window and dust. A shallow puddle of water reflects the bike’s front wheel and the glowing blue headlights.

The bike is angled fifteen degrees to the left, showing both the aggressive skull fairing and the exposed engine cutaway on the left side. The icy‑blue headlights are active, casting two distinct beams that cut through the mist and illuminate the marble floor ahead, creating a path of light. The blue energy core pulses visibly through the ribcage cover. The cloak panel hangs behind the rear wheel, its segmented edges glowing.

The lighting is cinematic: a key light from the upper right (the eclipse’s corona) casts warm but pale light on the bike’s right side. A fill light from the blue arcane mist provides soft, cool ambience from below. The bike’s own LEDs act as rim lights, outlining its sharp edges. The result is a three‑dimensional, volumetric rendering that makes the bike feel both heavy and agile, ancient and futuristic.

The bike shows natural imperfections: a tiny chip in the paint on the left fairing, a faint smudge on the windshield, micro‑scratches on the bronze trim, and a thin layer of dust on the lower engine casing. These details reinforce the hyper‑realistic mandate.

Lower Quarter — Typography and Calligraphic Elements

Arranged as an integrated calligraphic composition over the lowest portion of the image, placed so it does not obscure the bike’s wheels or ground reflection. The typography is rendered as sharp, high‑resolution text with subtle metallic textures and drop shadows, not hand‑drawn, but styled to evoke traditional Japanese seal and calligraphy forms (digitally perfected).

Hero’s Name (Bike Model):
“FISSION VOID” lettered in large, semi‑calligraphic English text — thick‑and‑thin brush‑inspired strokes, but rendered in cold blue with a faint silver outline. The letters are spaced wide, commanding.

Formal Title (below name):
“THE DEMON HUNTER’S STEED” rendered slightly smaller, same calligraphic style but more restrained, soft silver color.

Hanko Seal (below title):
An inked vermilion square seal, slightly imperfect edges as if hand‑carved, but rendered in photorealistic 4K. The seal contains a stylized skull crossed with a sword — Alucard’s personal emblem adapted for the bike.

Catchphrase (below seal):
“The darkness does not flee. It is executed.” Lettered in a smaller, elegant serif font with a slight italic tilt, faded silver with a blue glow.

Dialogue Quote (below catchphrase):
“They branded my father a traitor. I will carve his honor into the Abyss itself.” Rendered in a slightly different typography — a clean, futuristic sans‑serif with a subtle metallic texture — to distinguish it as spoken or thematic text. Soft white with a faint blue drop shadow.

Entire Image Mood: Epic, dark, vengeful, and undeniably cinematic. A main poster for a futuristic demon‑hunter hyperbike — the kind that would be printed on metal and hung in a collector’s garage.

CHAPTER PANEL TRACKING LOG
Not applicable — no panels in Prologue.

SPEECH BUBBLE AND TYPOGRAPHY GUIDE
Not applicable — no speech bubbles. Dialogue is presented as integrated typography in the lower calligraphic section.

ABSOLUTELY FORBIDDEN ELEMENTS — SAFETY COMPLIANCE
This prompt contains none of the following: explicit violence, blood, gore, dismemberment, corpses, realistic wounding, body horror, graphic death imagery, sexual content, nudity, or age‑inappropriate content. The afterimages depict mechanical components — no injury, no enemies, no combat impacts. The bike is depicted with full dignity and no human rider. No character appears. No religious symbols that could be offensive (the cathedral is fictional fantasy gothic, not real‑world denomination). No real brand logos.

TECHNICAL SPECIFICATIONS FOR IMAGE GENERATION
Aspect Ratio: 9:16 (vertical)

Art Style: Photorealistic cinematic rendering (as defined in Sacred Mantra)

Format: Single unified full-bleed composition — no panels, no gutters

Resolution: 8K (7680x13653 pixels equivalent), also compatible with 4K and HD downscales

Render Style: Hyper‑detailed raw photo simulation with anamorphic lens characteristics (oval bokeh, slight horizontal lens flare). ARRI Alexa 65 look.

Camera & Lens: 85mm prime lens at f/1.8, focused on the central bike. Shallow depth of field — background cathedral ruins and afterimages slightly soft but legible.

Lighting: Cinematic, high‑contrast. Key light from upper right (eclipse corona). Fill light from blue arcane mist below. Rim light from bike’s own LEDs. The bike’s headlights cast visible beams through the mist.

Texture Requirements: Subsurface scattering on translucent materials (engine cover, LEDs). Visible micro‑scratches on painted metal, faint smudges on windshield, dust particles suspended in the air, natural imperfections (small chip, thin dust layer). The leather seat shows subtle grain and faint wear.

Film Grain: 5% intensity, 35mm film grain pattern overlay.

Color Grading: ARRI Look Library “Dark Moon” custom profile — cold shadows (deep navy), desaturated midtones, icy blue highlights. Black levels crushed but retain detail. No oversaturation of any color except the blue LEDs, which are allowed to glow.

Calligraphy & Typography Integration: Text elements are overlaid with subtle drop shadows and metallic textures, perfectly sharp. No hand‑drawn artifacts. The calligraphic forms are digitally rendered but inspired by seisho/reisho/gyosho/keisho brush aesthetics.

Aspect and Edge Finishing: A thin black border (2% width) with a very faint inner blue glow. No deckled edges — clean, modern, cinematic.





TITLE OF THE SERIES
MALEFIC STAR — The Shining Star’s Chariot

CHAPTER / PIECE TITLE
Prologue — The Cannon from the Skies

IMAGE TYPE IDENTIFICATION
Single unified full-bleed 9:16 poster composition. No panels. No gutters. One continuous image with the visual and emotional force of a futuristic techno‑mythic tapestry rendered in hyper‑realistic, cinematic photography.

ART STYLE DECLARATION — SACRED MANTRA
4K, 8K, HD, UHD, hyperdetailed, raw photo, cinematic lighting, anamorphic lens, sharp focus, intricate surface texture, subsurface scattering on painted metal and translucent materials, visible micro‑scratches and natural imperfections, faint peach fuzz on leather surfaces, film grain, color graded, ARRI Alexa look, 85mm lens, f/1.8 aperture, masterpiece quality.

The visual identity is that of a high‑budget cinematic advertisement for a futuristic hypercar — every surface rendered with obsessive fidelity. The lighting is bright and crisp, with soft highlights that kiss the car’s elegant curves and deep shadows that define its aggressive, long‑range stance. The color grading leans into a warm, clean palette with crisp white, royal blue, radiant gold, and accents of crystalline teal from the energy core and headlights. Film grain is present at 5% intensity, evenly distributed, adding texture without degrading the hyper‑detailed finish. The image must feel as though it was captured on a cinema camera with an anamorphic lens — slight oval bokeh in out‑of‑focus background elements, a subtle horizontal lens flare, and a shallow depth of field that keeps the car razor‑sharp while the floating city behind it softens into atmospheric brightness. Every material reads as real: polished carbon fiber, brushed aluminum, warm leather, translucent glass over LEDs, and the faint shimmer of energy particles rising from the Malefic Core.

The following elements are absolutely forbidden: 90s anime, cel animation, retro anime, VHS grain, scanlines, hand‑drawn line art, cel shading, pixelation, vector art, low resolution, flat colors, or any descriptor that suggests non‑photorealistic rendering.

CHARACTER REFERENCES — COMPLETE AND EXHAUSTIVE
Not applicable — no character appears. The supercar is the sole subject. The following car design references are locked for all elements of the image.

Primary Car — MALEFIC STAR (Central Figure)

Overall Silhouette: A low, wedge‑shaped, mid‑engine hypercar with a long wheelbase and a stretched, aerodynamic teardrop cabin. The stance is wide and planted, suggesting stability at extreme range. The car blends elegant steampunk ornamentation with futuristic energy weapon aesthetics — a fusion of a Victorian pocket watch and a railgun.

Color Palette: Crisp white as the primary body color. Royal blue for accents, interior trim, and underbody glow. Radiant gold for structural frames, wheel details, and the Malefic Core housing. Crystalline teal for all lighting (headlights, core, taillights, rim LEDs, afterimage glows).

Front View: The front fascia is dominated by a wide, low grille shaped like a stylized star burst — a series of gold radiating lines from a central teal emblem. Two vertically stacked headlights on each side glow with crystalline teal energy, each housing multiple small LEDs that mimic a star field. The hood is long and sculpted, with two sharp creases that run from the grille to the windshield, framing a central gold trim line. The front splitter is carbon fiber with gold inlays, shaped like a pair of elegant wings. The windshield is a seamless, dark blue tinted glass that reflects the sky.

Side Profile: The profile is defined by an elongated, teardrop cabin that flows into a sharply tapered tail. Two massive wing‑like rear fins extend from the roof pillars, sweeping backward and ending in glowing teal energy emitters — these fins mimic Layla’s twin‑tails. The fins are made of polished white carbon fiber with royal blue undersides. The side air intakes are shaped like elongated crescents, trimmed in gold. The door is a scissor‑style unit, with a gold handle shaped like a small wand. Behind the door, a transparent gold‑ribbed glass cover reveals the Malefic Core — a mid‑mounted engine that pulses with azure light. The exhaust is a single, massive cannon‑shaped outlet centered at the rear (visible from side as a long barrel protruding between the twin fins). The wheels are pushed to the corners for maximum stability.

Rear View: The tail is dominated by the twin fins that rise and then sweep back, each fin ending in a teal LED emitter that casts a soft glow on the road. Between the fins, the Malefic Cannon exhaust is the focal point — a long, segmented gold barrel that appears to float, held by invisible magnetic fields, with a glowing teal ring at its tip. Below the cannon, a full‑width taillight bar of crystalline teal LEDs pulses slowly. The rear diffuser is massive, made of carbon fiber with gold vertical strakes. The license plate recess is integrated into the diffuser, shaped like a book (homage to Eruditio’s scholarly nature).

Engine Detail (used in afterimage): The Malefic Core is a mid‑mounted, quad‑turbocharged electric‑hybrid V8, but its aesthetic is steampunk‑futuristic. The engine block is finished in royal blue with gold cooling fins. The cam covers are silver with engraved star maps. Through the transparent gold‑ribbed glass cover, the central component is a spherical energy core glowing with crystalline teal, suspended by three gold prongs. High‑voltage cables wrapped in braided gold sheath snake away from the core.

Wheels and Tires: Five spoke‑less star‑shaped rims — each rim is a solid disc with five curved cutouts that form a five‑pointed star. The rims are finished in white with a gold outer lip. The edges of the rim have a continuous crystalline teal LED strip. Tires are low‑profile performance radials (Michelin Pilot Sport Cup 2 style) with a single teal pinstripe. Brake calipers are six‑piston units painted royal blue, gripping carbon‑ceramic discs with star‑shaped drill holes.

Lighting Consistency: All crystalline teal lighting (headlights, core, taillight, rim LEDs, fin emitters) shares the same cool, bright blue‑green hue — hex approximate #00E5FF — with a subtle pulsing effect (10% brightness oscillation) that mimics the rhythm of a heartbeat or energy discharge. The headlights also feature a “long‑range” mode where the light projects in a narrow, focused beam.

Interior Detail (visible through windshield – optional but encouraged): The cabin is minimalist and high‑tech. Two seats wrapped in white leather with royal blue stitching. A holographic heads‑up display projects speed and targeting data onto the windshield. The steering wheel is a yoke style, finished in gold and white, with teal backlighting.

PANEL‑BY‑PANEL NARRATIVE, DIALOGUE AND SPEECH BUBBLES
Not applicable — no panels. The following describes the single unified composition from top to bottom.

The Canvas (9:16 vertical):
Upper Quarter — The Afterimage Array (Car Part Studies)

Filling the upper portion of the background, from approximately the top edge down to the main car’s roof level, are four ghosted afterimages of the Malefic Star’s critical components. These afterimages are rendered semi‑transparent (approximately 40% opacity), floating in the bright air like holographic schematics or projected data from the City of Scholars. Each afterimage is labeled with small, elegant calligraphic text integrated into the composition (sharp, high‑resolution, not hand‑drawn). Each afterimage shows a different part of the car in exquisite detail.

Afterimage 1 (far left) — The Malefic Core Engine:
A close‑up, cutaway view of the mid‑mounted engine. The spherical teal energy core is visible suspended inside a gold cage. Quad turbos with gold housings flank the core. Blue coolant lines with tiny droplets.
Label (calligraphic, small, floating below the afterimage): “MALEFIC CORE — Quad‑Turbo Electric‑Hybrid V8”

Afterimage 2 (center‑left) — Star Burst Grille & Headlights:
A tight frontal view of the car’s nose. The star burst grille radiates gold lines. The twin stacked headlights on each side glow teal, each a miniature star field. The carbon fiber splitter with gold winglets is visible below.
Label: “STAR BURST GRILLE — Active Aero & LED Matrix”

Afterimage 3 (center‑right) — Twin‑Tail Fins & Cannon Exhaust:
A rear three‑quarter close‑up of the car’s tail. The two wing‑like rear fins sweep back, their teal emitters glowing. The segmented gold cannon exhaust barrel floats between them, with a teal energy ring at the tip.
Label: “TWIN‑TAIL FINS & MALEFIC CANNON — Active Aerodynamics / Energy Discharge”

Afterimage 4 (far right) — Star Rims & Brake Assembly:
A macro view of the front wheel. The spoke‑less star‑shaped rim with five curved cutouts. The teal LED rim strip glows. The royal blue six‑piston caliper grips a carbon‑ceramic disc with star‑shaped holes.
Label: “STAR RIM — Forged Magnesium / Self‑Illuminating”

All afterimages are positioned so they do not obstruct the central car but instead frame it, creating a sense of technical reverence and futuristic elegance.

Middle‑Upper Background — Eruditio’s Floating City (Photorealistic Rendering)

Behind and between the afterimages, the background is a hyper‑realistic vista of Eruditio, the City of Scholars — a floating steampunk metropolis under a bright, warm sun. The city consists of multiple circular platforms connected by glass bridges, with towering spires of white stone and gold filigree. The Spire of Knowledge rises in the distance, its peak surrounded by floating rings of blue energy. Airships with brass propellers drift between buildings. The sky is a perfect gradient from pale blue at the horizon to a soft golden haze near the sun. Sunbeams pierce through gaps in the floating architecture, creating dramatic shafts of light.

The platform where the car is parked is a pristine white laboratory terrace on the edge of the city, overlooking a vast sky and distant floating islands. The terrace has a polished white floor with gold inlaid grid lines, and a low glass railing etched with scholarly equations. A few small golden orbs (hovering drones) float near the railing, recording data. The overall mood is bright, scholarly, and optimistic — a city of science, wonder, and long‑range precision.

Lower Two‑Thirds — The Central Figure (Malefic Star Supercar)

The car is parked at the center of the white laboratory terrace, approximately in the lower two‑thirds of the frame, slightly angled toward the viewer (three‑quarter front view). The front grille and twin headlights face slightly left, while the side profile reveals the twin‑tail fins and the mid‑engine cover. The car’s white body gleams under the bright sun, with soft reflections of the floating city on its hood. The crystalline teal headlights are active, casting two focused beams that stretch forward into the distance, disappearing over the terrace edge. The Malefic Core pulses gently through the gold‑ribbed glass cover. The twin‑tail fins cast sharp shadows on the white floor.

The lighting is bright and crisp: the sun from above right provides warm key light. The white floor reflects soft fill light upward. The car’s own teal LEDs provide cool rim lighting on the edges of the fins and wheels. The result is a clean, high‑key, futuristic look with just enough shadow to define the car’s complex curves.

The car shows natural imperfections: a tiny chip on the front splitter’s gold inlay, a faint water spot on the windshield, micro‑scratches on the door handle, and a thin layer of dust on the rear diffuser (from air travel). These details reinforce the hyper‑realistic mandate.

Lower Quarter — Typography and Calligraphic Elements

Arranged as an integrated calligraphic composition over the lowest portion of the image, placed so it does not obscure the car’s wheels or ground reflection. The typography is rendered as sharp, high‑resolution text with subtle metallic textures and drop shadows, not hand‑drawn, but styled to evoke traditional Japanese seal and calligraphy forms (digitally perfected).

Hero’s Name (Car Model):
“MALEFIC STAR” lettered in large, semi‑calligraphic English text — thick‑and‑thin brush‑inspired strokes, rendered in radiant gold with a faint teal glow. The letters are spaced wide, elegant, commanding.

Formal Title (below name):
“THE SHINING STAR’S CHARIOT” rendered slightly smaller, same calligraphic style but more restrained, soft white with royal blue shadows.

Hanko Seal (below title):
An inked vermilion square seal, slightly imperfect edges as if hand‑carved, but rendered in photorealistic 4K. The seal contains a stylized cannon crossed with a star — Layla’s personal emblem adapted for the car.

Catchphrase (below seal):
“From the skies, I watch. From the skies, I strike.” Lettered in a smaller, elegant serif font with a slight italic tilt, faded silver with a teal glow.

Dialogue Quote (below catchphrase):
“My parents built an airship to touch the stars. I built this to protect the city that remembers them.” Rendered in a slightly different typography — a clean, futuristic sans‑serif with a subtle metallic texture — to distinguish it as spoken or thematic text. Soft white with a faint teal drop shadow.

Entire Image Mood: Bright, hopeful, precise, and undeniably cinematic. A main poster for a futuristic scholar‑warrior hypercar — the kind that would be projected on a giant screen at an auto show in the clouds.

TITLE OF THE SERIES
LUNAR AEGIS — The Priestess of the Moon’s Chariot

CHAPTER / PIECE TITLE
Prologue — The Arrow of Eclipse

IMAGE TYPE IDENTIFICATION
Single unified full-bleed 9:16 poster composition. No panels. No gutters. One continuous image with the visual and emotional force of a celestial, moonlit tapestry rendered in hyper‑realistic, cinematic photography.

ART STYLE DECLARATION — SACRED MANTRA
4K, 8K, HD, UHD, hyperdetailed, raw photo, cinematic lighting, anamorphic lens, sharp focus, intricate surface texture, subsurface scattering on painted metal and translucent materials, visible micro‑scratches and natural imperfections, faint peach fuzz on leather surfaces, film grain, color graded, ARRI Alexa look, 85mm lens, f/1.8 aperture, masterpiece quality.

The visual identity is that of a high‑budget cinematic advertisement for a futuristic lunar‑themed hypercar — every surface rendered with obsessive fidelity. The lighting is cool and dramatic, with soft, moon-like highlights that kiss the car’s elegant curves and deep, velvety shadows that define its mysterious, elven silhouette. The color grading leans into a cold, ethereal palette with midnight blue, silver, pale lilac, and accents of piercing icy‑blue from the energy core and headlights. Film grain is present at 5% intensity, evenly distributed, adding texture without degrading the hyper‑detailed finish. The image must feel as though it was captured on a cinema camera with an anamorphic lens — slight oval bokeh in out‑of‑focus background elements, a subtle horizontal lens flare, and a shallow depth of field that keeps the car razor‑sharp while the lunar temple behind it softens into atmospheric mystery. Every material reads as real: polished carbon fiber, brushed silver, warm leather, translucent glass over LEDs, and the faint shimmer of moon particles rising from the Lunar Aegis core.

The following elements are absolutely forbidden: 90s anime, cel animation, retro anime, VHS grain, scanlines, hand‑drawn line art, cel shading, pixelation, vector art, low resolution, flat colors, or any descriptor that suggests non‑photorealistic rendering.

CHARACTER REFERENCES — COMPLETE AND EXHAUSTIVE
Not applicable — no character appears. The supercar is the sole subject. The following car design references are locked for all elements of the image.

Primary Car — LUNAR AEGIS (Central Figure)

Overall Silhouette: A low, wide, mid‑engine hypercar with a crescent‑shaped profile. The front is pointed and sharp like an arrowhead, the cabin is a smooth teardrop, and the rear tapers into twin, bow‑like fins. The stance is poised and ready, like an archer drawing a bowstring. The car blends elven elegance with high‑tech lunar weaponry — a fusion of a silver bow and a spaceship from a celestial temple.

Color Palette: Midnight blue (almost black in shadows) as the primary body color. Silver for structural elements, trim, and the crescent grille. Pale lilac for subtle accents and interior glow. Icy blue for all lighting (headlights, core, taillights, rim LEDs, afterimage glows, particle streams).

Front View: The front fascia is dominated by a wide, low grille shaped like a crescent moon bow — a sweeping silver arc that curves upward at both ends, with a central icy‑blue emblem of a crescent moon. Two twin headlights are stacked vertically on each side, each housing a miniature crescent‑shaped LED that glows with piercing icy‑blue light. The hood is long and sleek, with two silver creases that run from the grille to the windshield, framing a faint lilac pinstripe. The front splitter is carbon fiber with silver winglets, shaped like arrowheads. The windshield is a dark, blue‑tinted glass that reflects the starry sky.

Side Profile: The profile is defined by a long, sweeping teardrop cabin that flows into a sharply tapered tail. The side mirrors are not ordinary — they are silver wing‑like structures that extend backward and upward, mimicking Miya’s elongated elven ears. Each mirror has a thin icy‑blue LED strip along its edge. The side air intakes are shaped like elongated crescent slashes, trimmed in silver. The door is a scissor‑style unit with a silver handle shaped like an arrow. Behind the door, a transparent silver‑ribbed glass cover reveals the Lunar Aegis Core — a mid‑mounted engine that pulses with cold celestial light. The roof has a crystalline tiara‑shaped spoiler — a small, fixed wing with three pointed peaks, each tipped with a glowing icy‑blue crystal. The exhaust outlets are at the rear: two stacked bow‑limb‑shaped pipes that curve like the ends of a longbow, each trailing faint, glowing blue particle streams that dissipate into the air.

Rear View: The tail is dominated by the twin bow‑limb exhaust outlets, which are made of polished silver with pale lilac inner linings. Between them, a full‑width taillight bar in a thin, continuous icy‑blue crescent glows softly. Below the exhaust, a massive carbon fiber diffuser with silver vertical strakes mimics the fletching of an arrow. The license plate recess is integrated into the diffuser, shaped like an arrowhead. The rear fenders flare slightly, housing the wide rear wheels.

Engine Detail (used in afterimage): The Lunar Aegis Core is a mid‑mounted, twin‑turbocharged electric‑hybrid V6, but its aesthetic is moon‑touched. The engine block is finished in pale lilac with silver cooling fins shaped like crescent moons. The cam covers are silver with engraved lunar phases. Through the transparent silver‑ribbed glass cover, the central component is a spherical, moon‑like energy core glowing with icy‑blue light, suspended by three silver prongs. Glowing blue coolant lines snake away, and small floating moon‑dust particles orbit the core.

Wheels and Tires: Six‑spoke wheels, but each spoke is shaped like an arrow fletching — a long, tapered triangle with three feathered grooves. The rims are finished in silver with a midnight blue inner barrel. The rim edges have a continuous icy‑blue LED strip. Tires are low‑profile performance radials (Pirelli P Zero style) with a single silver pinstripe. Brake calipers are six‑piston units painted pale lilac, gripping carbon‑ceramic discs with crescent‑moon‑shaped drill holes.

Interior Detail (visible through windshield – optional but encouraged): The cabin is minimal and celestial. Two seats wrapped in pale lilac leather with silver stitching. A holographic heads‑up display projects moon phases and a targeting reticle onto the windshield. The steering wheel is a yoke style, finished in silver and midnight blue, with icy‑blue backlighting.

Lighting Consistency: All icy‑blue lighting (headlights, core, taillight, rim LEDs, mirror strips, particle trails) shares the same cold, bright blue hue — hex approximate #4DF8FF — with a subtle pulsing effect (10% brightness oscillation) that mimics the rhythm of a heartbeat or the glow of moonlight reflecting off ice. The headlights also feature a “moonbeam” mode where the light projects in a soft, diffused cone.

PANEL‑BY‑PANEL NARRATIVE, DIALOGUE AND SPEECH BUBBLES
Not applicable — no panels. The following describes the single unified composition from top to bottom.

The Canvas (9:16 vertical):
Upper Quarter — The Afterimage Array (Car Part Studies)

Filling the upper portion of the background, from approximately the top edge down to the main car’s roof level, are four ghosted afterimages of the Lunar Aegis’s critical components. These afterimages are rendered semi‑transparent (approximately 40% opacity), floating in the cold night air like holographic projections from the Moon Temple. Each afterimage is labeled with small, elegant calligraphic text integrated into the composition (sharp, high‑resolution, not hand‑drawn). Each afterimage shows a different part of the car in exquisite detail.

Afterimage 1 (far left) — The Lunar Aegis Core Engine:
A close‑up, cutaway view of the mid‑mounted engine. The spherical moon‑like energy core glows icy‑blue, suspended in a silver cage. Twin turbos with silver housings flank the core. Glowing blue coolant lines with tiny droplets.
Label (calligraphic, small, floating below the afterimage): “LUNAR AEGIS CORE — Twin‑Turbo Electric‑Hybrid V6”

Afterimage 2 (center‑left) — Crescent Bow Grille & Headlights:
A tight frontal view of the car’s nose. The crescent moon bow grille sweeps in a silver arc. Twin stacked headlights on each side, each a miniature crescent LED glowing icy‑blue. The arrowhead front splitter is visible below.
Label: “CRESCENT BOW GRILLE — Active Aero / Crescent LED Matrix”

Afterimage 3 (center‑right) — Elven Ear Mirrors & Tiara Spoiler:
A side close‑up showing the silver wing‑like side mirror (elven ear shape) with its icy‑blue edge light. Above, the crystalline tiara‑shaped roof spoiler with three glowing blue peaks.
Label: “ELVEN EAR MIRRORS & TIARA SPOILER — Aerodynamic / Lunar Crystal Array”

Afterimage 4 (far right) — Arrow Fletching Wheels & Brake Assembly:
A macro view of the front wheel. The six arrow‑fletching spokes radiate from the hub. The icy‑blue rim LED strip glows. The pale lilac six‑piston caliper grips a carbon‑ceramic disc with crescent‑moon‑shaped holes.
Label: “ARROW FLETCHING WHEEL — Forged Magnesium / Self‑Illuminating Rim”

All afterimages are positioned so they do not obstruct the central car but instead frame it, creating a sense of lunar reverence and elven craftsmanship.

Middle‑Upper Background — Moonlit Temple & Celestial Sky (Photorealistic Rendering)

Behind and between the afterimages, the background is a hyper‑realistic vista of a Moonlit Temple — a vast, circular ritualistic dais made of polished dark stone, inlaid with glowing, neon‑blue rings of energy that slowly rotate. The dais is surrounded by a misty, low‑lying fog that blankets the ground. Rising from the mist are massive, skeletal structures of white stone — resembling both ribs and gothic arches — that seem to pierce the sky. These are the ruins of an ancient elven sanctuary. In the distance, jagged mountain peaks are silhouetted against a deep indigo sky that fades into a lighter cyan at the horizon. The sky is dotted with countless faint stars and a massive, glowing full moon that casts a silver-blue light across the entire scene. Small glowing particles of blue light drift around the dais and the car, like moon dust or magic.

The dais itself is the platform where the car is parked. It has a polished, mirror‑like surface that reflects the car’s underbody and the glowing rings. The rings are made of energy, not solid — they hover just above the stone, pulsing slowly. The overall mood is mystical, solemn, and powerful — a sacred space where a guardian prepares for battle.

Lower Two‑Thirds — The Central Figure (Lunar Aegis Supercar)

The car is parked at the center of the dark stone dais, approximately in the lower two‑thirds of the frame, angled slightly toward the viewer (three‑quarter front view). The crescent bow grille and twin headlights face slightly right, while the side profile reveals the elven ear mirrors, the tiara spoiler, and the mid‑engine cover. The car’s midnight blue body gleams under the moon, with soft reflections of the starry sky on its hood. The icy‑blue headlights are active, casting two soft, moonbeam‑like cones that fade into the mist ahead. The Lunar Aegis core pulses gently through the silver‑ribbed glass cover. The twin bow‑limb exhaust outlets trail faint blue particle streams that drift upward like glowing smoke.

The lighting is cool and dramatic: the full moon from above and slightly behind provides a silver backlight. The glowing blue rings on the dais provide rim light from below. The car’s own icy‑blue LEDs provide cool front lighting. The result is a high‑contrast, moonlit look with deep shadows that define the car’s elegant curves and sharp edges.

The car shows natural imperfections: a tiny chip on the front splitter’s silver inlay, a faint water spot on the windshield from morning dew, micro‑scratches on the mirror housing, and a thin layer of mist condensation on the rear diffuser. These details reinforce the hyper‑realistic mandate.

Lower Quarter — Typography and Calligraphic Elements

Arranged as an integrated calligraphic composition over the lowest portion of the image, placed so it does not obscure the car’s wheels or the dais’s glowing rings. The typography is rendered as sharp, high‑resolution text with subtle metallic textures and drop shadows, not hand‑drawn, but styled to evoke traditional Japanese seal and calligraphy forms (digitally perfected).

Hero’s Name (Car Model):
“LUNAR AEGIS” lettered in large, semi‑calligraphic English text — thick‑and‑thin brush‑inspired strokes, rendered in silver with a faint icy‑blue glow. The letters are spaced wide, elegant, commanding.

Formal Title (below name):
“THE PRIESTESS OF THE MOON’S CHARIOT” rendered slightly smaller, same calligraphic style but more restrained, soft white with pale lilac shadows.

Hanko Seal (below title):
An inked vermilion square seal, slightly imperfect edges as if hand‑carved, but rendered in photorealistic 4K. The seal contains a stylized bow crossed with a crescent moon — Miya’s personal emblem adapted for the car.

Catchphrase (below seal):
“By the light of the moon, I draw. By the will of the moon, I loose.” Lettered in a smaller, elegant serif font with a slight italic tilt, faded silver with a soft blue glow.

Dialogue Quote (below catchphrase):
“For a thousand years I watched from the temple. Now the Abyss expands again. The moon’s arrow will fly.” Rendered in a slightly different typography — a clean, futuristic sans‑serif with a subtle metallic texture — to distinguish it as spoken or thematic text. Soft white with a faint icy‑blue drop shadow.

Entire Image Mood: Mystical, solemn, powerful, and undeniably cinematic. A main poster for a futuristic elven moon priestess hypercar — the kind that would be displayed in a sacred temple’s holographic archive.

TECHNICAL SPECIFICATIONS FOR IMAGE GENERATION
Aspect Ratio: 9:16 (vertical)

Art Style: Photorealistic cinematic rendering (as defined in Sacred Mantra)

Format: Single unified full-bleed composition — no panels, no gutters

Resolution: 8K (7680x13653 pixels equivalent), also compatible with 4K and HD downscales

Render Style: Hyper‑detailed raw photo simulation with anamorphic lens characteristics (oval bokeh, slight horizontal lens flare). ARRI Alexa 65 look.

Camera & Lens: 85mm prime lens at f/1.8, focused on the central car. Shallow depth of field — background lunar temple and afterimages slightly soft but legible.

Lighting: Cool, dramatic moonlight. Key light from above and behind (full moon). Fill light from glowing blue dais rings below. Rim light from car’s own icy‑blue LEDs. The headlights cast soft, diffused moonbeams into the mist.

Texture Requirements: Subsurface scattering on translucent materials (engine cover, LEDs, crystal tiara). Visible micro‑scratches on midnight blue paint, faint smudges on windshield, mist condensation on body panels, dust particles floating in the moonlight. The leather interior shows subtle grain through the window.

Film Grain: 5% intensity, 35mm film grain pattern overlay.

Color Grading: ARRI Look Library “Moonlit Night” custom profile — cool highlights (icy blue), deep midnight shadows, desaturated midtones with a hint of lilac. High contrast, low brightness, rich blacks with detail.

Calligraphy & Typography Integration: Text elements are overlaid with subtle drop shadows and metallic textures, perfectly sharp. No hand‑drawn artifacts. The calligraphic forms are digitally rendered but inspired by seisho/reisho/gyosho/keisho brush aesthetics.

Aspect and Edge Finishing: A thin silver border (2% width) with a very faint inner icy‑blue glow. No deckled edges — clean, modern, cinematic.




TITLE OF THE SERIES
THUNDER’S WRATH — The Lightning Weaver’s Stormhawk

CHAPTER / PIECE TITLE
Prologue — The Forked Lightning

IMAGE TYPE IDENTIFICATION
Single unified full-bleed 9:16 poster composition. No panels. No gutters. One continuous image with the visual and emotional force of a cold, electric mythic tapestry rendered in hyper‑realistic, cinematic photography.

ART STYLE DECLARATION — SACRED MANTRA
4K, 8K, HD, UHD, hyperdetailed, raw photo, cinematic lighting, anamorphic lens, sharp focus, intricate surface texture, subsurface scattering on painted metal and translucent materials, visible micro‑scratches and natural imperfections, film grain, color graded, ARRI Alexa look, 85mm lens, f/1.8 aperture, masterpiece quality.

The visual identity is that of a high‑budget cinematic advertisement for a futuristic electric‑attack helicopter — every surface rendered with obsessive fidelity. The lighting is cold and dramatic, with sharp, high‑contrast highlights that define the helicopter’s jagged edges and deep, icy shadows that emphasize its lethal elegance. The color grading leans into a frigid, high‑voltage palette with deep navy, crisp white, and glowing cyan from the energy core, rotor fields, and weapon pods. Film grain is present at 5% intensity, evenly distributed, adding texture without degrading the hyper‑detailed finish. The image must feel as though it was captured on a cinema camera with an anamorphic lens — slight oval bokeh in out‑of‑focus background elements, a subtle horizontal lens flare, and a shallow depth of field that keeps the helicopter razor‑sharp while the arctic wasteland behind it softens into atmospheric cold. Every material reads as real: brushed titanium, carbon fiber, translucent energy containment glass, and the faint electric ozone scent implied by the arcing particles.

The following elements are absolutely forbidden: 90s anime, cel animation, retro anime, VHS grain, scanlines, hand‑drawn line art, cel shading, pixelation, vector art, low resolution, flat colors, or any descriptor that suggests non‑photorealistic rendering.

CHARACTER REFERENCES — COMPLETE AND EXHAUSTIVE
Not applicable — no character appears. The helicopter is the sole subject. The following helicopter design references are locked for all elements of the image.

Primary Helicopter — THUNDER’S WRATH (Central Figure)

Overall Silhouette: A sleek, narrow, single‑rotor attack helicopter with a distinctly predatory, lightning‑bolt‑shaped fuselage. The nose is sharp and pointed like a stiletto, the cockpit is a narrow teardrop, and the tail boom tapers into four pointed, fork‑like stabilizers arranged in a fan shape. The helicopter is low to the ground on jagged, crystal‑like landing skids, with a slight nose‑down hover attitude.

Color Palette: Deep navy (almost black in shadows) as the primary fuselage color. Crisp white for the cockpit frame, tail boom accents, and rotor hub. Glowing cyan for all energy effects (rotor fields, engine core, weapon arcs, insignia, and running lights).

Rotor System (Unique Feature): The main rotor does not have solid metal blades. Instead, four translucent cyan energy blades extend from the hub, each shaped like a jagged lightning bolt. These blades are semi‑transparent, with crackling electricity arcing between them. The rotor hub is a polished silver‑white dome with a glowing cyan core. The tail rotor is a smaller, two‑bladed energy disc, also cyan, with visible electrical discharge.

Front View: The nose is a sharp, angular wedge. Two piercing, almond‑shaped headlights glow with arcing cyan energy — each headlight is a cluster of miniature LEDs that flicker like lightning. Between them, a narrow air intake shaped like a jagged fork. The cockpit canopy is a dark, polarized blue‑grey glass with a faint cyan reflection. Below the nose, a chin‑mounted sensor turret with a single glowing cyan eye.

Side Profile: The fuselage is long and slender, with sharp, faceted panels that mimic the jagged edges of a lightning bolt. On the side of the fuselage, a neon‑cyan lightning bolt insignia runs from just behind the cockpit to the engine exhaust. The engine is a mid‑mounted Thunder’s Wrath Core visible through a translucent, armored glass cover — inside, a spherical capacitor array with visible blue arcing between terminals. Stub wings extend from the lower fuselage, each carrying two lightning‑cannon pods (four total). The pods are cylindrical, with a central glowing cyan ring and a forward emitter that trails electric arcs. The tail boom is a thin, hexagonal tube that flares into four fork‑like stabilizers — two horizontal, two vertical, each tipped with a small cyan LED. The landing skids are not tubular but faceted, resembling jagged ice crystals, painted white with cyan edge lights.

Rear View: The four fork stabilizers are the most striking feature — they fan out like the fingers of a hand or the prongs of a fork, each with a sharp, metallic point. Between them, the tail rotor is a glowing cyan disc of energy. The exhaust outlet is a single, vertical slit that pulses with cyan light. The rear of the fuselage has a small, triangular taillight bar.

Engine Detail (used in afterimage): The Thunder’s Wrath Core is a hybrid electric‑plasma turbine. The central component is a spherical capacitor made of translucent blue ceramic, inside which a miniature lightning storm is visible — bolts of cyan energy jump between inner electrodes. Surrounding the sphere are six copper‑colored magnetic rings that pulse in sequence. High‑voltage cables in braided silver sheath snake to the rotor hub and weapon pods.

Weapon Pods (used in afterimage): Four identical lightning cannons mounted under the stub wings. Each pod is 1.5 meters long, with a dark navy casing and a polished silver emitter nozzle. The nozzle has three prongs that arc electricity between them when active. A small targeting sensor on the pod’s side glows cyan.

Landing Skids: Designed to look like jagged ice crystals — multi‑faceted, with sharp angles, finished in white with thin cyan LED strips along each edge. They are low‑profile, keeping the helicopter close to the ground in hover.

Lighting Consistency: All cyan lighting (rotor blades, headlights, engine core, weapon pods, stabilizer tips, insignia) shares the same cold, bright blue‑green hue — hex approximate #00FFFF — with a flickering effect (randomized brightness oscillation between 80% and 100%) to simulate lightning. The headlights project narrow, focused beams that cut through the cold air.

Lower Two‑Thirds — The Central Figure (Thunder’s Wrath Helicopter)

The four energy rotor blades are in motion — captured with slight motion blur, their cyan arcs creating a luminous disc above the fuselage. The twin headlights are active, casting narrow, focused beams that cut through the cold mist and illuminate the runes on the platform below. The landing skids (ice‑crystal style) hover just above the stone, with faint cyan reflections beneath.

The lighting is cold and dramatic: the low sun from the upper left provides a harsh, pale backlight that outlines the helicopter’s sharp edges. The glowing platform runes provide fill light from below, casting cyan upward onto the fuselage. The helicopter’s own cyan lights (rotor, engine, headlights) provide rim and front lighting. The result is a high‑contrast, arctic, electrified look with deep shadows that define every facet of the helicopter’s jagged surfaces.

The helicopter shows natural imperfections: a tiny scuff on the left stub wing’s leading edge, a faint frost pattern on the cockpit canopy, micro‑scratches on the silver rotor hub, and a thin layer of ice crystals on the landing skids (from the freezing environment). These details reinforce the hyper‑realistic mandate.

Arranged as an integrated calligraphic composition over the lowest portion of the image, placed so it does not obscure the helicopter’s skids or the platform’s glowing runes. Hero’s Name (Helicopter Model):
“THUNDER’S WRATH” lettered in large, semi‑calligraphic English text — thick‑and‑thin brush‑inspired strokes, rendered in glowing cyan with a faint silver outline. The letters are spaced wide, commanding, electric.

Catchphrase (below seal):
“I do not summon the storm. I am the storm.” Lettered in a smaller, elegant serif font with a slight italic tilt, faded silver with a cyan glow.

TITLE OF THE SERIES
ORBITING SWORD — The Wandering Sword’s Steed

CHAPTER / PIECE TITLE
Prologue — The Wandering Blade

IMAGE TYPE IDENTIFICATION
Single unified full-bleed 9:16 poster composition. No panels. No gutters. One continuous image with the visual and emotional force of a cold, cyber‑ronin mythic tapestry rendered in hyper‑realistic, cinematic photography.

ART STYLE DECLARATION — SACRED MANTRA
4K, 8K, HD, UHD, hyperdetailed, raw photo, cinematic lighting, anamorphic lens, sharp focus, intricate surface texture, subsurface scattering on painted metal and translucent materials, visible micro‑scratches and natural imperfections, film grain, color graded, ARRI Alexa look, 85mm lens, f/1.8 aperture, masterpiece quality.

The visual identity is that of a high‑budget cinematic advertisement for a futuristic hovering heavy assault bike — every surface rendered with obsessive fidelity. The lighting is dramatic and cold, with sharp, high‑contrast highlights that define the bike’s thick armor plates and deep, ominous shadows that emphasize its silent, predatory nature. The color grading leans into a desolate, high‑tech palette with cerulean blue, slate grey, and crimson from the power core, visor, and energy effects. Film grain is present at 5% intensity, evenly distributed, adding texture without degrading the hyper‑detailed finish. The image must feel as though it was captured on a cinema camera with an anamorphic lens — slight oval bokeh in out‑of‑focus background elements, a subtle horizontal lens flare, and a shallow depth of field that keeps the bike razor‑sharp while the fragmented laboratory behind it softens into atmospheric ruin. Every material reads as real: brushed titanium, carbon fiber, armored plating, translucent energy glass, and the faint ozone scent implied by the plasma thrusters.

The following elements are absolutely forbidden: 90s anime, cel animation, retro anime, VHS grain, scanlines, hand‑drawn line art, cel shading, pixelation, vector art, low resolution, flat colors, or any descriptor that suggests non‑photorealistic rendering.

CHARACTER REFERENCES — COMPLETE AND EXHAUSTIVE
Not applicable — no character appears. The bike is the sole subject. The following bike design references are locked for all elements of the image.

Primary Bike — ORBITING SWORD (Central Figure)

Overall Silhouette: A heavily armored, hovering superbike with a broad, aggressive stance. The bike has no wheels — it floats above the ground on four silent anti‑gravity thrusters. The fuselage is thick and angular, resembling a samurai’s armored chest plate. The front is a wide, battle‑mask fairing. The tail rises into two sharp, blade‑like fins. The overall shape is low, wide, and menacing — a cybernetic war machine designed for silent, precise elimination.

Color Palette: Cerulean blue as the primary armor color. Slate grey for structural elements and thrusters. Crimson for all lighting (visor headlight, power core, thruster glow, ribbon LED, afterimage glows). Matte black for flexible joint covers and underbody.

Front View: The front fairing is shaped like a samurai’s menpō (face mask) — broad cheek plates, a central nose ridge, and a horizontal slit where the visor sits. The single crimson visor‑style headlight spans the entire width of the fairing, glowing with a cold, unwavering red light — no eyes, no expression, just a lethal stare. Below the visor, a narrow air intake shaped like a grimacing mouth. The fairing is made of interlocking cerulean armor plates with slate grey trim and subtle silver rivets. The front fender (above the forward thrusters) is split into two claw‑like points.

Side Profile (Left Side): The fuel tank is heavily armored with overlapping cerulean plates, each edged in slate grey. At the center of the tank, a triangular red power core pulses slowly, visible through a transparent crimson cover. Behind the tank, the seat is a single carbon fiber unit with dark grey gripper fabric. The tail section flares into two upward‑swept blade‑like fins — sharp, angular, and finished in cerulean with crimson inner edges. Between the fins, a thin red LED ribbon trails backward, floating in the air as if caught in a silent wind. The engine is a mid‑mounted Orbiting Sword unit — a circular housing with five small energy blades that orbit slowly around the crankcase, each blade shaped like a miniature katana, glowing red. The exhaust is a twin‑pipe system exiting below the tail: each pipe is shaped like a katana hilt (square tsuba‑style guard, wrapped grip texture), venting faint crimson energy wisps. Under the chassis, four anti‑gravity thrusters are visible: two in front, two in back. Each thruster is a flat, disc‑shaped unit with a slate grey housing and a glowing crimson plasma ring at the center. The bike hovers approximately 30 cm above the ground.

Rear View: The two blade fins are the most striking feature — they rise from the tail at a 45‑degree angle, sharp and dangerous. The red LED ribbon hangs between them, a thin line of light. The taillight is a narrow crimson slit just below the fins. The twin katana‑hilt exhaust outlets protrude downward. The rear thrusters are visible, glowing faintly.

Engine Detail (used in afterimage): The Orbiting Sword unit is a circular electromagnetic ring housing five floating energy blades. Each blade is 30 cm long, curved like a katana, made of translucent red energy with a visible white‑hot edge. They orbit the crankcase at a constant speed, held in place by magnetic fields. The central crankcase is slate grey with cerulean cooling fins. High‑voltage cables in braided silver sheath connect the unit to the power core and thrusters.

Anti‑Gravity Thrusters (used in afterimage): A close‑up of one thruster. It is a flat, circular disc, 40 cm in diameter, with a slate grey outer ring and a dark grey inner core. The plasma ring glows crimson, with faint ripples of heat distortion above it. Tiny blue sparks occasionally jump from the ring.

Tail Fins & Ribbon (used in afterimage): A detailed view of the tail section. The two blade fins are made of forged cerulean carbon fiber with razor‑sharp edges. The red LED ribbon is a flexible, fiber‑optic strip that glows evenly, trailing 50 cm behind the bike.

Lighting Consistency: All crimson lighting (visor headlight, power core, orbiting blades, thrusters, taillight, ribbon) shares the same deep, cold red hue — hex approximate #FF0033 — with a steady, unwavering glow (no pulsing, to reflect Saber’s emotionless precision). The visor headlight projects a narrow, horizontal beam forward, cutting through the dark.

Hover Effect: The bike has no contact with the ground. It floats silently. The thrusters emit no sound, only a faint heat shimmer. Dust and debris on the laboratory floor are undisturbed directly under the bike, but a faint red glow is cast onto the floor from the thrusters.

PANEL‑BY‑PANEL NARRATIVE, DIALOGUE AND SPEECH BUBBLES
Not applicable — no panels. The following describes the single unified composition from top to bottom.

The Canvas (9:16 vertical):
Upper Quarter — The Afterimage Array (Bike Part Studies)

Filling the upper portion of the background, from approximately the top edge down to the main bike’s top fairing level, are four ghosted afterimages of the Orbiting Sword’s critical components. These afterimages are rendered semi‑transparent (approximately 40% opacity), floating in the dark void like holographic combat schematics from Laboratory 1718. Each afterimage is labeled with small, elegant calligraphic text integrated into the composition (sharp, high‑resolution, not hand‑drawn). Each afterimage shows a different part of the bike in exquisite detail.

Afterimage 1 (far left) — The Orbiting Sword Engine Unit:
A close‑up, cutaway view of the circular engine housing. Five translucent red energy blades orbit a slate grey crankcase. Magnetic field lines are faintly visible as blue arcs between the blades.
Label (calligraphic, small, floating below the afterimage): “ORBITING SWORD UNIT — Electromagnetic Blade Array / 5x Floating Katanas”

Afterimage 2 (center‑left) — Samurai Mask Fairing & Visor:
A tight frontal view of the bike’s nose. The menpō‑shaped fairing with broad cheek plates, central ridge, and the single crimson visor headlight spanning the width. The grimacing mouth intake is visible below.
Label: “MENPŌ FAIRING — Reactive Armor / Crimson Visor LED Array”

Afterimage 3 (center‑right) — Blade Fins & LED Ribbon:
A rear three‑quarter close‑up of the tail section. The two upward‑swept blade fins, sharp and angular. The red LED ribbon trails behind, a thin line of constant light.
Label: “BLADE FINS & RIBBON — Aerodynamic Stabilizers / Fiber‑Optic Trail”

Afterimage 4 (far right) — Anti‑Gravity Thruster:
A macro view of one of the four thrusters. The flat, circular disc with slate grey housing, dark grey core, and glowing crimson plasma ring. Faint heat shimmer above the ring.
Label: “ANTI‑GRAVITY THRUSTER — Plasma Lift System / Silent Operation”

All afterimages are positioned so they do not obstruct the central bike but instead frame it, creating a sense of cold, technical autopsy — as if the viewer is studying a weapon’s blueprint.

Middle‑Upper Background — Fragmented Laboratory & Dark Void (Photorealistic Rendering)

Behind and between the afterimages, the background is a hyper‑realistic depiction of the ruins of Laboratory 1718 — a vast, dark void punctuated by floating fragments of metal, broken catwalks, and shattered glass. The floor below is a patchwork of cracked, blood‑stained white tiles and exposed wiring. In the distance, the silhouettes of broken cryo‑chambers and sparking computer banks are barely visible through the darkness. The only light sources are the bike’s own crimson glow and a faint, cold overhead light that casts long, sharp shadows. Faint red digital glitches — thin, horizontal artifacts — drift across the background like corrupted data. Tiny floating particles (dust, ash, or pixel fragments) catch the crimson light. The overall mood is one of abandonment, violence, and cold, mechanical emptiness — the birthplace of a cyborg killer, now silent.

Lower Two‑Thirds — The Central Figure (Orbiting Sword Hover Bike)

The bike hovers approximately 30 cm above the fragmented laboratory floor, centered in the lower two‑thirds of the frame, angled slightly toward the viewer (three‑quarter front view from the right). The samurai mask fairing faces left, while the side profile reveals the heavy armor plates, the triangular red power core, the orbiting sword engine unit, and the twin katana‑hilt exhausts. The single crimson visor headlight is active, casting a narrow, horizontal beam that cuts through the dark void and illuminates a patch of cracked tiles ahead. The five orbiting energy blades are in motion — captured with slight motion blur, their red trails forming a circular halo around the engine. The bike’s four anti‑gravity thrusters glow faintly, casting a soft red reflection on the floor below. The red LED ribbon trails behind the tail fins, floating in the still air.

The lighting is cold and dramatic: a single overhead light (moonlight or emergency lamp) provides a pale, harsh top light that outlines the bike’s armored contours. The bike’s own crimson lights (visor, core, thrusters, blades, ribbon) provide rim and fill lighting from below and sides. The result is a high‑contrast, noir‑cyberpunk look with deep shadows that define every thick armor plate and sharp edge.

The bike shows natural imperfections: a faint scorch mark on the left thruster housing, a small scratch on the cerulean fairing near the visor, a thin layer of dust on the tail fins, and a single smudge on the triangular power core’s transparent cover. These details reinforce the hyper‑realistic mandate.

Lower Quarter — Typography and Calligraphic Elements

Arranged as an integrated calligraphic composition over the lowest portion of the image, placed so it does not obscure the bike’s thrusters or floor reflection. The typography is rendered as sharp, high‑resolution text with subtle metallic textures and drop shadows, not hand‑drawn, but styled to evoke traditional Japanese seal and calligraphy forms (digitally perfected).

Hero’s Name (Bike Model):
“ORBITING SWORD” lettered in large, semi‑calligraphic English text — thick‑and‑thin brush‑inspired strokes, rendered in crimson with a faint silver outline. The letters are spaced wide, cold, commanding.

Formal Title (below name):
“THE WANDERING SWORD’S STEED” rendered slightly smaller, same calligraphic style but more restrained, soft slate grey with cerulean shadows.

Hanko Seal (below title):
An inked vermilion square seal, slightly imperfect edges as if hand‑carved, but rendered in photorealistic 4K. The seal contains a stylized katana crossed with a circuit board — Saber’s personal emblem adapted for the bike.

Catchphrase (below seal):
“The sword remembers. The sword always remembers.” Lettered in a smaller, elegant serif font with a slight italic tilt, faded silver with a crimson glow.

Dialogue Quote (below catchphrase):
“They erased my name. They erased my memories. They erased everything that made me human. But they could not erase the blade.” Rendered in a slightly different typography — a clean, futuristic sans‑serif with a subtle metallic texture — to distinguish it as spoken or thematic text. Soft white with a faint red drop shadow.

Entire Image Mood: Cold, precise, mournful, and undeniably cinematic. A main poster for a futuristic cyborg ronin hover bike — the kind that would be displayed in a dystopian tech gallery or a weapon manufacturer’s showroom.

TECHNICAL SPECIFICATIONS FOR IMAGE GENERATION
Aspect Ratio: 9:16 (vertical)

Art Style: Photorealistic cinematic rendering (as defined in Sacred Mantra)

Format: Single unified full-bleed composition — no panels, no gutters

Resolution: 8K (7680x13653 pixels equivalent), also compatible with 4K and HD downscales

Render Style: Hyper‑detailed raw photo simulation with anamorphic lens characteristics (oval bokeh, slight horizontal lens flare). ARRI Alexa 65 look.

Camera & Lens: 85mm prime lens at f/1.8, focused on the central bike. Shallow depth of field — background laboratory ruins and afterimages slightly soft but legible.

Lighting: Cold, dramatic, high‑contrast. Key light from above (single overhead lamp). Fill light from bike’s own crimson LEDs and thruster glow. The visor headlight casts a narrow horizontal beam.

Texture Requirements: Subsurface scattering on translucent materials (energy blades, power core cover, thruster plasma rings). Visible micro‑scratches on cerulean paint, scorch marks on thrusters, dust on fins, smudge on core cover. Floating dust/ash particles in the air.

Film Grain: 5% intensity, 35mm film grain pattern overlay.

Color Grading: ARRI Look Library “Cybernetic Noir” custom profile — cold highlights (cerulean), deep slate shadows, crimson accents. High contrast, medium brightness, rich blacks with detail.

Calligraphy & Typography Integration: Text elements are overlaid with subtle drop shadows and metallic textures, perfectly sharp. No hand‑drawn artifacts. The calligraphic forms are digitally rendered but inspired by seisho/reisho/gyosho/keisho brush aesthetics.

Aspect and Edge Finishing: A thin crimson border (2% width) with a very faint inner slate grey glow. No deckled edges — clean, modern, cinematic.




TITLE OF THE SERIES
HEAVY SPIN — The Panda Warrior’s Rolling Fortress

CHAPTER / PIECE TITLE
Prologue — The Unmovable Boulder

IMAGE TYPE IDENTIFICATION
Single unified full-bleed 9:16 poster composition. No panels. No gutters. One continuous image with the visual and emotional force of a mythic, nature‑infused war machine tapestry rendered in hyper‑realistic, cinematic photography.

ART STYLE DECLARATION — SACRED MANTRA
4K, 8K, HD, UHD, hyperdetailed, raw photo, cinematic lighting, anamorphic lens, sharp focus, intricate surface texture, subsurface scattering on painted metal and translucent materials, visible micro‑scratches and natural imperfections, film grain, color graded, ARRI Alexa look, 85mm lens, f/1.8 aperture, masterpiece quality.

The visual identity is that of a high‑budget cinematic advertisement for a futuristic eco‑warrior main battle tank — every surface rendered with obsessive fidelity. The lighting is warm and dappled, with soft sunbeams filtering through bamboo canopy, creating dramatic highlights on the tank’s white armor and deep, earthy shadows that emphasize its massive, grounded presence. The color grading leans into a natural, vibrant palette with crisp white, deep charcoal black, emerald green, bamboo tan, and warm orange from the power core accents. Film grain is present at 5% intensity, evenly distributed, adding texture without degrading the hyper‑detailed finish. The image must feel as though it was captured on a cinema camera with an anamorphic lens — slight oval bokeh in out‑of‑focus background elements, a subtle horizontal lens flare, and a shallow depth of field that keeps the tank razor‑sharp while the misty bamboo forest behind it softens into atmospheric serenity. Every material reads as real: composite armor, bamboo‑reinforced plating, rubberized tracks, glowing energy conduits, and the faint scent of petrichor and ozone implied by the floating spore lights.

The following elements are absolutely forbidden: 90s anime, cel animation, retro anime, VHS grain, scanlines, hand‑drawn line art, cel shading, pixelation, vector art, low resolution, flat colors, or any descriptor that suggests non‑photorealistic rendering.

CHARACTER REFERENCES — COMPLETE AND EXHAUSTIVE
Not applicable — no character appears. The tank is the sole subject. The following tank design references are locked for all elements of the image.

Primary Tank — HEAVY SPIN (Central Figure)

Overall Silhouette: A massive, heavily armored main battle tank with a broad, rounded turret and a low, wide hull. The tank blends modern military design with organic, nature‑inspired elements. The overall shape is bulky and imposing, yet friendly — like a giant panda made of steel and bamboo. The tank has six large road wheels on each side, covered by thick side skirts woven from bamboo‑composite material. The front glacis is sloped and rounded, painted white with black shoulder‑like reactive armor blocks.

Color Palette: Crisp white as the primary armor color. Deep charcoal black for reactive armor blocks, track skirts, and turret accents. Emerald green for all lighting (headlights, power core, cannon energy rings, spore lights, and afterimage glows). Bamboo tan for structural trim and the main cannon wrap. Warm orange for the gourd‑shaped power core.

Front View: The front of the hull is shaped like a panda’s face — wide, rounded, with two large emerald‑green LED headlights arranged as the “eyes,” each set inside a black “eye patch” panel. A prominent, dark, V‑shaped snout (the driver’s vision block cluster) sits between the headlights, painted matte black. Above the headlights, a small, friendly “eyebrow” ridge in white. The front glacis is sloped at 60 degrees, made of white composite armor with subtle bamboo‑grain texture. Below the snout, a narrow, smiling “mouth” air intake grille with vertical bamboo slats. The tank has no visible gun mantlet — the main cannon emerges from a circular opening in the upper front hull, just above the snout. Two tow hooks are shaped like small bamboo shoots.

Side Profile (Left Side): The hull is long and low, with six large road wheels (rubber‑rimmed, black) and a raised drive sprocket at the front, idler at the rear. The tracks are wide, with deep chevron treads for soft terrain. The side skirts are made of woven bamboo‑composite armor — overlapping vertical slats of tan and green, tied with red cord lacing, giving a traditional East Asian aesthetic. Above the skirts, the hull side is white with black diagonal stripes (panda shoulder motif). The turret is rounded, almost dome‑like, sitting slightly off‑center to the rear. On the turret side, a large glowing orange gourd‑shaped power core is visible through a transparent green crystal cover — this is the “magatama” charm that powers the tank. The main cannon is a single, long bamboo‑stalk barrel — segmented, with natural nodes, wrapped in thick red cord along its length. The barrel is not smooth; it has a textured, organic look, as if grown rather than forged. At the muzzle, a glowing emerald green energy ring hovers, firing energy shells. The commander’s cupola on top of the turret has a small green frog figurine (cosmetic mascot) attached to a short pole, painted in bright green with a tiny red hat. The exhaust vents at the rear are shaped like bamboo leaves, venting soft green steam.

Rear View: The rear hull is armored with a large, rounded white plate. The exhaust outlets are two vertical slots, each emitting a faint green vapor. A rear‑mounted flail‑like anti‑mine device hangs from a hydraulic arm — a heavy, spinning hammer with four weighted heads, painted black with red accents. The device is retracted when not in use, resting against the rear armor. The tow pintle is shaped like a coiled bamboo root.

Engine Detail (used in afterimage): The engine is a hybrid electric‑biofuel turbine, housed under the rear deck. The deck has bamboo‑pattern cooling grilles (slotted tan metal) with small green LED indicators. The power plant is a “Nature Core” — a spherical chamber where bio‑luminescent algae and nanites generate electricity, visible through a small inspection window as a swirling green glow.

Track & Road Wheels (used in afterimage): A close‑up of the running gear. Six dual‑wheel road wheels per side, with rubber tires and green hubcaps. The track links are wide, with chevron treads and small bamboo‑shaped cleats. The side skirt is partially lifted to reveal the wheels.

Bamboo Cannon & Energy Ring (used in afterimage): A macro view of the main gun. The bamboo barrel shows natural segmentation and slight weathering. The red cord wrapping is tight and even. At the muzzle, the emerald green energy ring is perfectly circular, with faint electrical arcs jumping to the barrel.

Lighting Consistency: All emerald green lighting (headlights, cannon ring, spore lights, engine glow) shares the same vibrant, warm green hue — hex approximate #00FF88 — with a slow, breathing pulse (10% brightness oscillation) to mimic natural life energy. The orange power core pulses at a different rhythm, like a heartbeat.

Nature Integration: The tank is not sterile — it has small glowing spore lights (floating particles) drifting around it, and tiny bamboo shoots growing from crevices in the side skirts, suggesting the machine is alive or blessed by nature. The tank’s armor has a slight bamboo‑grain texture on the white panels, achieved through micro‑etching.

Tiny bamboo shoots are visible growing from the side skirt crevices, and glowing spore lights drift around the tank. The small green frog figurine on the commander’s cupola catches a sunbeam, its tiny red hat bright.

The lighting is warm and natural: the sun from above‑right filters through bamboo, creating dappled light patterns on the tank’s white armor. Fill light comes from the mist and the glowing spore lights. The tank’s own green and orange lights provide soft rim lighting on the edges. The result is a harmonious, high‑detail image that blends modern military hardware with natural serenity.

The tank shows natural imperfections: a few fallen bamboo leaves resting on the front glacis, a small smear of mud on the left track skirt, micro‑scratches on the headlight lenses, and a tiny chip on the rear flail hammer. These details reinforce the hyper‑realistic mandate.
